利率上行引發波動，惟無損 AI 供應鏈擴張動能
2026 年 10 月
9 月全球股市普遍回落，主因為油價急升、全球長端殖利率攀高及主要央行轉鷹。僅有日本股市
受 AI、半導體需求與企業信心支撐，抵銷 BOJ 升息壓力，成為主要市場中唯一正報酬。標普 500 9
月小跌 0.3%，雖 AI 與大型科技股獲利韌性仍在，但 Fed 於 9 月升息並暗示年底前再度升息，加上
油價與美債殖利率走高，壓抑估值並引發科技股震盪。印度股市下跌 6.9%最弱，主要受油價飆升、
全球利率上行及外資單月淨流出約 27 億美元拖累。中國股市下跌 4.7%，因受內需修復偏弱、科技

股評價承壓所致；歐洲股市也因油價單月大漲近 14%與全球殖利率攀升影響，9 月下跌 4.6%。
9 月主要債市全面下跌，核心壓力來自全球殖利率急升。美歐日央行同步升息、Brent 原油單月
上漲約 14%、 美國經濟數據偏強與公債供給疑慮 ， 使美國10 年期殖利率單月上升約 53bp，創 2022
年以來最大升幅。主要券種中，全球投資級公司債下跌 2.93%表現最弱，新興美元債亦下跌 2.91%，
因 9 月全球公債殖利率快速攀升，此兩類債券對利率變化均較敏感 ； 其中投資級公司債相較高收益債
存續期較長、票息緩衝較低，因此在利率重新定價期間承受較大價格壓力。亞洲高收益債下挫 1.20%
相對抗跌，因高票息與較短存續期提供緩衝；新興當地債跌 1.74%，亦優於多數美元債種。
在股市方面，AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器 DRAM、先進製程與封裝等關鍵供
應鏈維持成長動能，市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分化。若AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資
本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整

體而言 ， 科技產業與台灣及日本股市獲利表現仍具關注度 ， 惟後續仍須留意全球景氣 、 產業供需變化 、
政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
就債市而言，雖然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期，但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶
來的排擠效應，以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率
環境下，中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級
公司債指數利差約 80 個基點 ， 雖然利差偏窄 ， 但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準 。 因此，
在目前市場環境下 ， 中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別 ， 惟後續表現仍須
留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

美國股市：通膨與升息預期上升推動美債利率上漲，美股呈高檔區間震盪
9 月因美伊關係緊張升溫 ， 推升油價並加劇市場對通膨回升的擔憂 ；相較於 6 月點陣圖 ，9 月FOMC
官員全面上修 2026 至 2027 年的政策利率預估至 4.1%，並暗示 2026 年底前會再升息一次，加上美
國經濟數據表現優於預期，使美國公債殖利率走升，進而壓抑美股表現。在此背景下，9 月標普 500
指數呈現高檔區間震盪格局。產業表現方面，資訊科技與通訊服務類股相對強勢，持續扮演指數上漲
的主要動能；相較之下，醫療保健、必需消費及部分工業類股表現較為疲弱。

9 月美國聯準會貨幣政策會議以 12 比 0 的表決結果 ， 將聯邦基金利率目標區間上調25 個基點至
3.75%到 4.00%。9 月最新經濟預測，美國聯準會官員上修 2026 年核心 PCE 至 3.4%，2027 年維持
在 2.5%，將 2026-2027 年經濟成長上調 0.1%至 2.3%與 2.4%，顯示聯準會認為美國經濟與通膨仍
具韌性。
觀察美國近期的經濟數據可以發現企業投資動能依然強健，元大投顧表示，美國8 月扣除飛機與
國防的核心資本財訂單由前期的月增 0.6%加速至 1.6%， 其中初級金屬 、 機械及電機設備訂單均呈現
增長，顯示 AI 相關投資及企業設備更新需求持續支撐資本財訂單，企業設備投資可望對經濟成長形
成穩健貢獻。
自 6 月底以來 ， 市場持續上修標普500 指數企業獲利預估 ， 其中以科技與能源類股貢獻最為明顯 ，
反映企業營運表現優於市場預期。AI 投資循環持續推進、企業數位轉型需求增加，以及美國內需維
持穩健，共同支撐企業獲利成長動能，並有助於吸收高利率環境帶來的壓力。與此同時，標普 500

指數前瞻本益比已由先前高點回落，評價壓力較先前明顯緩解。隨著企業獲利持續改善，美股行情正
由過去仰賴資金與情緒推升評價 ， 逐步轉向由基本面支撐股價 ， 後續表現將更取決於企業獲利是否持
續優於預期。
投資策略方面，投資焦點已逐漸由評價擴張轉向企業獲利成長與基本面表現。短期市場仍須留意
政策面不確定性 ， 美中貿易談判進展將牽動全球供應鏈與科技產業展望 ， 若關稅或科技限制措施再度
升溫，可能對科技股評價造成壓力；此外，美國期中選舉逐漸接近，企業稅率、科技監管及產業政策
等議題亦可能影響市場風險偏好。整體而言，企業獲利改善仍是支撐美股最重要的力量，若 AI 投資
循環與獲利成長趨勢延續，美股中長期表現仍可望由基本面驅動。

歐洲股市：儘管企業獲利上修，仍需持續關注地緣政治發展
9 月泛歐 600 指數下跌約 2%，產業表現方面 ，製藥 、生技、銀行及部分科技類股表現相對強勢，
顯示資金偏好具獲利能見度與防禦特性的族群 ； 相較之下 ， 礦業 、 消費及部分金融服務類股表現落後 ，
反映市場對歐洲經濟成長及企業獲利前景仍持審慎態度。整體而言，受到高油價以及高利率環境，投
資氣氛轉趨保守，市場資金持續聚焦於基本面較穩健的產業。
歐洲央行 9 月政策利率由 2.25% 升息 1 碼至 2.50 % ，符合市場預期。歐洲央行最新經濟預
測中上修中期通膨展望，2027 年消費者物價指數年增率由 2.3% 升至 2.5% ，2028 年則由 2.0%

上調至 2.1% ；核心消費者物價指數方面，2027 年和 2028 年預測亦各上修 0.1 % 至 2.6% 及
2.3% ，能源成本上升對食品及其他價格的傳導，使得通膨回落速度較原先預期緩慢。ECB 總裁拉加
德表示能源價格上漲逐步傳導至核心及食品通膨，預期整體通膨在 2027 年上半年維持在高檔。
根據花旗歐洲獲利修正指數(Earnings Revision Index) ，歐洲企業獲利預估已連續上修並到近五
年高點，反映企業基本面持續改善。受惠於歐元區景氣穩步復甦、製造業景氣回升，以及企業資本支
出逐步回暖，工業、金融及景氣循環類股獲利展望同步改善。隨著市場對企業未來盈餘預期提高，有
助支撐歐股評價面與資金流入，並成為推動泛歐 600 指數表現的重要動能。
然而，自 3 月美伊衝突爆發以來，市場對能源供給中斷的擔憂持續升溫，帶動油價走高，進而增
加仰賴進口能源的歐洲企業成本壓力，並壓抑歐股相對表現。進入9 月後，俄羅斯與北約緊張關係再
度升溫，歐洲安全局勢不確定性提高，進一步加劇市場對能源供應與地緣政治風險的擔憂。若相關衝

突持續延燒，不僅可能推升能源價格與通膨壓力，也可能影響企業投資意願及市場風險偏好，進而對
歐洲企業獲利與股市表現形成壓力。
展望 10 月，歐股仍將受到高油價、地緣政治風險及歐洲央行維持偏鷹立場等因素影響，市場波
動性可能維持偏高。近期能源價格攀升推升通膨預期，使 ECB 傾向維持較高利率水準，不僅提高企
業融資成本，也限制股市評價進一步擴張的空間。此外，中東局勢持續緊張，以及俄羅斯與北約間的
緊張關係再度升高，均可能影響投資人風險偏好，增添歐洲金融市場不確定性。雖然歐元區經濟活動
仍維持溫和擴張，企業獲利預估亦持續改善，但在高利率與地緣政治風險尚未明顯緩解之前，市場情
緒仍偏向審慎，後續仍需持續關注能源價格、通膨走勢及地緣政治發展，作為判斷歐股能否重啟上漲
動能的重要觀察指標。

日本股市：升息落地，景氣展望佳，第四季半導體與商社企業獲利動能有望延續
9 月日本股市受升息預期及長天期公債殖利率走高影響，部分高評價股票出現震盪，但這波調整
較偏向政策轉折下的評價重估，而非企業基本面全面轉弱。日本銀行於 9 月 18 日決議將短期政策利
率目標提高至約 1.25%，使市場反覆討論的升息方向逐漸明朗。隨著政策落地，投資焦點有望由利率
變動轉回企業營運與獲利。從正面角度看，利率正常化反映日本經濟具備一定韌性，股價整理也有助
於消化先前累積的評價壓力。若後續企業財測保持穩健，9 月的震盪可望成為第四季行情重新蓄勢的

過程。
日本股市的中期支撐，來自薪資成長、內需改善與企業投資的相互帶動。2026 年春鬥最終統計
顯示，5,368 個工會的平均調薪幅度達 5.01%，連續第三年維持在 5%以上；可明確辨識的基本薪資
調升幅度亦達 3.50%。薪資持續增加，有助於提升家庭消費能力，為零售與服務等內需產業提供較穩
定的需求基礎。若企業能透過產品競爭力、定價能力及經營效率吸收成本，消費回溫便有機會轉化為
獲利成長。同時，全球 AI 相關投資也為日本半導體設備、載板及電子零組件供應鏈創造機會，使內
需復甦與科技需求形成雙重支撐。
展望 10 月，隨著升息議題逐步由預期轉為已知條件，市場可望更加重視第四季訂單、企業財測
及實際獲利。科技產業方面，若全球 AI 相關資本支出持續擴大，日本具技術優勢的設備與零組件企
業，有機會將需求轉化為接單及出貨成長；商社與資源相關企業，則可觀察能源、原物料業務及投資
收益對現金流的貢獻。內需產業亦值得留意薪資成長能否帶動消費，以及企業能否維持利潤率。整體
而言，10 月的投資機會不必侷限於單一題材，市場有望逐步從政策消息主導，轉向由產業需求與企

業業績支撐的行情。
投資策略方面，半導體供應鏈可優先挑選訂單能見度高、具技術門檻且獲利成長明確的企業；商
社則著重現金流、資本配置與股東回饋，內需股則觀察消費回溫能否反映在營收及利潤。若股價因利
率或匯率波動而短暫回落，但企業基本面與財測未明顯改變，可考慮分批布局，避免追逐短線漲幅。
後續仍須追蹤長天期利率 、 日圓及企業財測變化 ， 但投資主軸可更積極地放在具備實質成長能力的公
司。日本央行升息落地後，企業獲利能否接棒，將是日本股市延續表現的關鍵。

台灣股市：訂單持續暢旺突破千億，AI 需求持續向供應鏈擴散，廠商展望樂觀
9 月觀察台股基本面，最明確的支撐來自 8 月外銷訂單創下單月新高，以及企業對 9 月接單維持
擴張預期。經濟部公布，8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6 億美元，月增 5.1%、年增 71.4%；其中資訊通信
與電子產品合計 799.6 億美元，占整體 77.7%，顯示 AI 伺服器、半導體與雲端建設仍是主要成長來
源。受查廠商對 9 月接單的預期，以訂單金額計算的動向指數為 52.1，高於代表持平的 50，意味整
體接單有望較 8 月增加。雖然 9 月實際結果仍待公布，但已發布的數據與企業展望，為台股進入第

四季提供了正面的基本面依據。
更值得留意的是，AI 需求正從核心晶片延伸至更廣的供應鏈。雲端業者擴充運算能力，除了帶動
伺服器與半導體接單，也使自研 ASIC、IC 設計及相關電子零組件成為後續觀察重點；半導體投資與
設備擴產 ， 則為自動化設備 、 銅箔及高階材料創造機會 。8 月機械與基本金屬外銷訂單分別年增 28.3%
及 26.8%，顯示科技投資的效益已反映在部分非電子貨品。對台股而言，這有助於投資主線從少數大
型權值股，延伸至具技術優勢與訂單能見度的中上游企業。不過，面板需求仍偏弱，產業表現並非全
面同步，選股仍須辨別企業實際受惠程度。
展望 10 月，市場焦點可望轉向第三季財報、第四季營運展望，以及 9 月外銷訂單能否驗證接單
續強。就廠商對 9 月的預期而言，資訊通信與電子產品的訂單金額動向指數分別為 52.4 及 51.5，顯
示科技需求仍具支撐；基本金屬與化學品則達 59.0 及 61.0，呈現部分材料領域接單改善的期待。若
AI 伺服器出貨 、 自研晶片開發及半導體資本支出持續推進 ， 相關台灣供應鏈的營收與獲利有望受惠。

另一方面，機械與光學器材指數分別為 46.3 及 49.5，提醒市場不能將整體訂單成長視為所有產業同
步復甦，仍須以後續實績檢驗。
投資策略方面，10 月以「掌握 AI 主線、尋找擴散機會、重視獲利驗證」作為配置方向。核心部
位可留意伺服器、半導體及 IC 設計等需求較明確的企業，並觀察 ASIC 相關業務能否由開發逐步轉
化為穩定出貨；其他機會則可從設備、銅箔與高階材料中，篩選確實取得訂單的公司。選股不只看產
業接單增幅，更應比較個別企業的營收成長、毛利率與獲利展望，避免在評價已高時追逐短期題材。
整體而言，台股仍有基本面支撐，後續行情能否擴大，關鍵在於 AI 需求能否持續轉化為更多企業的
實際獲利。

中國股市：AI 產業規模擴張難掩獲利困境，產業進入變現考驗
9 月中國 AI 產業的討論，從模型能力與使用量，逐漸轉向更根本的問題：在高階晶片取得受限、
業者競爭激烈的環境下，龐大的投入能否形成可持續的回報。9 月中美高層會談後，雙方宣布建立
AI 相關對話機制，但公開成果未顯示高階 AI 晶片出口限制已放寬。政策溝通有所進展，並未立即改
變企業取得先進算力的條件 。 與此同時 ， 低價及開放權重策略持續降低AI 使用門檻 ， 帶動市場擴張，
也使企業更難單靠模型服務取得理想利潤。對投資人而言，9 月最重要的訊號不是單一產品的表現，

而是中國 AI 已進入需要同時回答算力效率、商業變現與資本回收的階段。
從整體結構來看，中國具備電力、製造基礎及廣泛應用場景，有利於 AI 以較低成本進入企業營
運與日常服務；但部署成本較低，不代表產業獲利便能同步提高。當業者以低價爭取用戶，使用量可
以迅速累積，收入卻可能跟不上基礎設施、研發及營運支出。以特定平台的觀察為例，DeepSeek 曾
取得可觀的代幣使用量，但相應支出占比明顯較低；這不能代表中國 AI 產業的整體財務表現，卻凸
顯「有人使用」與「願意付費」之間仍有距離。未來產業能否改善獲利，取決於企業是否找到具持續
需求的應用、建立合理收費方式，並避免同質化服務陷入反覆降價。
展望 10 月 ， 中國AI 的成長空間仍在 ， 但市場衡量進展的標準應由使用規模 ， 逐步轉向投入產出。
若 AI 更深入企業工作流程，相關需求有望延續；然而，需求增加後，業者仍須證明服務品質、運算
效率與客戶付費能同步改善 。 晶片限制使算力使用效率更加重要 ， 價格競爭則使收入品質成為另一項
考驗。Vercel 資料顯示，8 月開放權重模型在其平台占代幣使用量 56%，支出占比卻為 14%，說明

高使用量未必對應高支出；10 月宜觀察企業是否開始披露更清楚的付費需求與成本改善，而非只比
較流量或模型排名。
投資策略方面，對中國 AI 宜維持關注成長、審慎評估獲利的態度，避免把產業滲透率提高，直
接等同於所有企業的投資價值提升。評估重點可放在三個整體問題：第一，持續增加的算力與研發投
入，能否換來足夠的收入；第二，低價競爭下，企業能否留住付費客戶並維持合理利潤；第三，AI
應用能否從試用走向長期採購。具備穩定需求、成本控制能力及清楚收費模式的企業，較有機會在產
業整合中建立優勢。整體而言，中國 AI 並非缺少使用場景，而是仍須證明大規模使用可以轉化為可
持續的獲利，這將是 10 月觀察產業與相關市場表現的核心。

科技類股：AI 應用加速落地，光通訊與 Agent 入口迎來成長機會
AI 產業的成長主軸正由模型訓練延伸至推論與實際應用 ， 讓軟體服務與硬體建設形成相互帶動的
循環。當 AI 不再只回答問題，而能協助搜尋資訊、安排活動及完成跨平台任務，使用頻率與運算需
求有望提高。Meta 於 9 月推出個人 AI 助理 Muse，透過應用程式及 WhatsApp 接觸既有用戶，積
極爭取 AI 服務入口。對產業而言，Agent 普及不僅意味新的服務形式，也可能擴大雲端運算與資料
傳輸需求，使應用端的創新成為基礎設施升級的推力。投資機會可從使用者入口延伸至相關供應鏈。

在硬體端，AI 推論與多步驟任務若持續增加，資料中心內部的資料交換將更頻繁，高速且節能的
連接能力因而更具價值 。 光通訊除支援資料中心之間的傳輸 ， 也逐步朝機櫃及晶片附近的互連場景發
展 ， 拓展供應商的產品空間 。Lumentum 指出 ，AI 運算對速度與頻寬的要求提升 ， 帶動光連結需求，
並已在營收展望中反映相關成長 。 高速光模組與新型互連方案也持續推進 。 雖然部分新技術仍須經過
客戶導入與量產驗證，但既有產品需求與後續技術升級有望接續，讓光通訊成為 AI 基礎設施中值得
持續關注的環節。
在應用端，Muse 代表 AI 從被動回覆走向主動協助執行任務的嘗試。若 Agent 能替使用者完成
搜尋、比較、預訂與付款等流程，便利性提升有望促進使用黏著度，並為支付、交易與服務串接平台
創造新的合作機會。跨平台操作可能由單一入口整合，促使各產業重新思考客戶服務。 「Muse 對各
產業的影響」圖可呈現商業模式轉變的可能方向。隨著更多服務接入 Agent 生態系，使用情境有望
持續擴展；後續若任務完成率、使用留存及付費轉換逐步改善，應用端便有機會形成更明確的收入來

源，並進一步支持運算與傳輸投資。
投資策略方面，建議以「應用入口與基礎設施並重」掌握 AI 從訓練走向推論的成長機會。硬體
方面，可優先關注已切入主要客戶供應鏈、具備高速光模組與新型互連產品布局，且訂單能見度較佳
的業者。應用方面，則可追蹤具備用戶基礎、服務整合能力與清楚收費模式的平台，觀察 Agent 能
否從新產品熱度轉化為持續使用與付費需求 。 配置上可先以業績較明確的基礎設施業者為核心 ， 再逐
步增加應用平台的布局，兼顧當前需求與後續成長空間。整體而言，AI 應用愈深入日常生活與企業
流程 ， 入口平台與光通訊供應鏈愈有機會相互受惠 ； 選股關鍵仍是找出能將需求成長轉化為實際業績
的企業。

拉美市場：10 月巴西總統大選將登場，政治不確定性影響市場表現
9 月拉美市場維持震盪整理 。 一方面 ， 拉美各國通膨數據普遍低於市場預期 ，有利市場降息預期；
另一方面，巴西總統大選選情持續拉鋸，政治不確定性升溫則讓投資人保持觀望。展望 10 月，隨巴
西大選投票日逼近，政治因素仍可能使拉美市場短線波動加劇。
受到美伊衝突帶動油價上漲影響，市場原本擔心燃料與運輸成本增加，會讓拉美物價再度大幅上
漲，進而迫使拉美央行升息。然而，高盛分析指出，拉美市場目前的通膨率仍受到控制，當地經濟承
受能源漲價的能力 ， 比市場原先擔心的情況更好 。 其中一個重要原因是 ， 新興市場政府透過燃油補貼 、

稅制安排與零售價格管制，讓消費者實際感受到的價格漲幅相對有限。此外，上半年全球農產品供給
較為充裕，使食品價格漲幅偏低，也抵銷了部分能源漲價帶來的影響。由於拉美市場實際通膨壓力小
於原先市場預期，央行進一步大幅升息的必要性也隨之降低，高盛因此認為，市場目前對多數拉美經
濟體未來一至兩年的升息預期過於悲觀，並預期拉美各國央行在 2027 年較可能重新轉向降息。
不過，拉美多國經濟出現放緩跡象，亦可能壓抑股市上漲空間。智利央行於 9 月會議維持政策利
率 4.50%不變 ， 並將2026 年經濟成長預測下修至 0.25%至 0.75%， 低於6 月預估的 1.0%至 1.75%。
智利央行指出，第二季內需成長放緩，勞動市場及信心指標同步轉弱，加上燃料價格上漲侵蝕企業與
家庭所得，均對經濟活動形成壓力。巴西經濟亦出現放緩跡象，巴西央行於 9 月 16 日一致決議調降
Selic 政策利率 25 個基點至 13.75%，並指出景氣循環產業動能轉弱，且整體與核心通膨均已回落至
目標區間上限以下，為進一步降息提供空間。不過，巴西央行並未承諾下次會議是否將繼續降息，而

是強調將視未來經濟與通膨數據逐次評估。
另一方面，巴西總統大選進入白熱化階段則是當前影響市場最主要的因子。市場民調顯示，相較
7 月時現任總統魯拉在民調中普遍領先競爭者波索納洛約 5 至 8 個百分點 ，在 9 月的民調雙方差距已
縮小至 2 個百分點以內，選情不確定性仍高。若選舉最終由魯拉勝選，其支持財政擴張的政策，將可
能使市場對於巴西財政赤字問題的擔憂加劇，進而使巴西股市面臨較大壓力；而若波索納洛勝選，市
場普遍預期其能提出改善投資人信心的財政政策 ， 為巴西央行創造更多降息空間 ， 有利後續巴西股市
上行。
整體而言，儘管市場對拉美 各國當前升息預期可能過於悲觀，但目前拉美多國經濟成長同步放
緩，加上巴西總統大選選情膠著，選後財政政策方向不確定性高，在考量大選及政策方向不確定性的
狀況下，短線拉美市場波動恐將加劇。

能源類股：全球石油庫存持續下降，但中東石油出口已出現回升
9 月西德州原油期貨價格受美伊衝突升溫影響，一度於月中重新站上每桶 100 美元，其後隨衝突
趨緩，油價漲幅逐步收斂，月底約回落至90 美元附近，能源類股表現亦大致跟隨油價走勢。展望10
月，近期中東石油供應恢復速度不如預期，加上庫存持續下降，預期油價下檔仍具支撐。然而，市場
數據顯示，高油價及供應受阻已同步抑制石油需求，且中東各國原油出口持續回升，後續供應緊張情
況有望逐步改善。因此，雖然中東地緣政治風險仍將對油價形成支撐，但隨美伊重啟外交接觸，油價
進一步大幅上漲的空間預期相對有限。

受到美伊談判僵持影響，國際能源署（IEA）與美國能源資訊署（EIA）在 9 月月報均將中東石油
供應恢復的預期時間延後。IEA 將中東石油供應全面恢復的預期時間延至 2027 年，並將 2026 年全
球石油供應減幅，由上月預估的每日減少 430 萬桶擴大至 570 萬桶；EIA 則將中東石油供給恢復至
衝突前水準的時間，由 2027 年初延至第 2 季。
此外 ，受到中東供應受阻與油價偏高影響 ，IEA 亦將 2026 年全球石油需求預測下修每日 94 萬桶 ，
預估全年需求將較去年減少每日 250 萬桶，EIA 則預估全年石油需求將減少約每日 170 萬桶，石油
輸出國組織（OPEC）也將今年需求由上月預估的每日增加 60 萬桶下修至 40 萬桶。
受此影響，全球石油庫存持續下降。IEA 估計，8 月全球可觀察石油庫存減少 9,500 萬桶，自 2
月底以來已累計下降 5.07 億桶，顯示市場仍持續動用庫存填補缺口。EIA 也預期全球石油庫存將持
續下降至年底，因此將2026 年下半年布蘭特原油均價預測，由上月約每桶82 美元上調至 90 美元；

此外 ，隨著 2027 年中東出口與產量逐步恢復 ，IEA 預估明年全年布蘭特原油均價將回落至 74 美元。
另一方面，中東原油出口則已經出現回升跡象。路透社 9 月 28 日引述 Kpler 初步資料指出，沙
烏地阿拉伯與阿聯酋 9 月原油出口均出現上升 ， 帶動中東主要產油國當月原油日均出口量回升至每日
1,280 萬桶，創下 2 月戰爭爆發以來最高水準。其中，沙烏地阿拉伯9 月原油日均出口預估將達每日
540 萬桶，較 8 月的 244.6 萬桶增加逾一倍。由於沙國東西向輸油管線先前因攻擊受損，沙烏地計
畫將部分原先經紅海延布港出口的原油 ， 轉往波斯灣沿岸港口裝運 ， 帶動拉斯坦努拉港9 月日均出貨
量預估由 8 月的每日 92.9 萬桶增至 360 萬桶。
整體而言，中東供應恢復時程延後，加上全球石油庫存持續下降，短期仍有利支撐油價。然而，
石油需求預估同步下修 ， 且沙烏地阿拉伯與阿聯酋原油出口均已逐步回升 ，後續石油供應緊張程度可
望有所緩解。整體而言，儘管油價仍受中東地緣風險支撐，但隨美國期中選舉接近，近期美伊亦重啟

外交接觸，市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂可望逐步降溫，油價進一步大幅上漲空間相對有限。

### Page 10

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：升息預期攀升壓抑金價表現；財富配置及央行買盤支撐中長期需求
近期黃金價格表現較為疲弱，主要受到市場升息預期持續攀升，帶動美債殖利率走高，增加持有
黃金的機會成本，對於利率較為敏感的黃金 ETF 需求因此受到壓抑。然而，高盛研究指出，聯準會
近期決議升息雖然延長了市場高利率環境時間，但並不會進一步墊高利率終點 ， 加上全球央行持續購
金，以及投資人從財富配置角度增加黃金配置，均有助於支撐中期黃金需求。
高盛報告指出，聯準會於 9 月升息一碼，增加持有黃金的機會成本，使對利率敏感的黃金ETF 需
求下滑 ， 高盛因此將2026 年底金價預估由每盎司 4,900 美元下修至 4,650 美元 。 然而 ， 高盛也預估，

聯準會將於 2027 年 9 月至 2028 年 3 月期間降息三碼，使政策利率最終回到 3.25%至 3.5%區間，
故儘管聯準會 9 月升息延長了高利率環境，但實際並未墊高利率終點，因此來自黃金 ETF 的賣壓僅
會延後金價上漲時程，但並未改變高盛對黃金的偏多看法。
另一方面，央行買盤仍是金價支撐核心。高盛9 月報告預估，全球央行近三個月經季節調整後的
購金速度已上升至每月 91 噸，遠高於 2022 年前每月 17 噸的平均水準。高盛指出，這股需求主要
來自分散外匯儲備的考量，在 2022 年俄羅斯央行資產遭美國凍結後，各國央行更加重視儲備資產遭
制裁或凍結的風險，相較於對利率敏感的 ETF 資金，央行基於儲備分散而進行的購金，不易因短期
利率變動而轉向，因此即使聯準會升息，央行買盤仍有利支撐金價表現。
除了央行買盤外，花旗也從全球財富配置的角度看好黃金。花旗指出，全球每年新增黃金礦產供
應規模相當有限，若全球家庭額外將 0.1%的財富配置於黃金，就相當於約 4,459 噸黃金購買量，也

等於 1.2 年的新增礦產供應量。換言之，即使全球投資人僅小幅提高黃金配置比重，也可能創造遠超
過新增供給的需求。由於黃金供給短期內難以快速增加，市場往往需要透過金價上漲，吸引既有持有
人釋出黃金以滿足新增需求，因此資產配置需求增加可能對金價產生顯著推升影響。
不過，花旗也指出，黃金新增供給有限的特性雖有助於放大需求對金價的推升效果，但同時也可
能提高金價出現較劇烈修正的風險。花旗估計，2022 年以來的金價上漲已使既有黃金持有人的帳面
財富增加約 13 兆美元，若持有人將其中約 1 兆美元轉回股票、債券或現金，所形成的黃金賣壓便可
能超過目前全部實體黃金需求，故當持有人集中獲利了結時，金價也可能出現較劇烈的修正。
整體而言，在市場升息預期持續攀升的情況下，短期黃金ETF 需求可能受到壓抑，金價仍可能面
臨震盪 。 然而 ， 全球央行基於外匯儲備分散需求持續購金 ， 加上投資人從財富配置角度提高黃金配置 ，
均有助於支撐中期黃金需求。

### Page 11

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：聯準會升息穩定通膨預期，凸顯中天期美債與投資級債吸引力
美國 9 月聯準會貨幣政策會議以 12 比 0 全票通過 ， 將聯邦基金利率目標區間上調 25 個基點至
3.75%–4.00%。最新經濟預測同步將 2026 年核心 PCE 上修至 3.4%、GDP 成長率上修至 2.3%，且
全面上修 2026 至 2027 年政策利率預估至 4.1%，並暗示年底前仍有一次升息空間。9 月聯準會採取
預防性升息，主要是在避免通膨預期鬆動並維持政策公信力，確立高利率維持更久的政策基調。
美國政府於 9 月 30 日公布第二季國內生產毛額(GDP)終值，經季節調整後成長年率由 1.5%上修

至 2.2%，高於經濟學家預估的維持不變。第一季 GDP 季增年率也由 2.1%上修至 2.5%。其中，占
美國經濟活動逾 6 成的消費支出，第二季成長率由 3.4%上修至 3.8%，顯示美國消費動能強勁；元
大投顧指出，美國 8 月核心資本財訂單月增率加速至 1.6%，其中初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均
呈現增長，顯示 AI 相關投資及企業設備更新需求持續支撐資本財訂單，企業設備投資可望對經濟成
長形成穩健貢獻。在消費與企業動能表現強勁下，使市場重新評估美國經濟對通膨黏性的影響。
彭博 10 月 1 日的報告指出，在 Market Pulse 調查的 173 名受訪者，超過一半的人預測美國 30
年期公債殖利率在年底將達到 6%。儘管伊朗戰爭和高借貸成本持續影響 ， 美國經濟仍維持強勁成長 ，
這種韌性加劇了通膨和進一步升息的⾵險；人工智慧基礎設施建設競賽引發了企業發債的狂潮 ，推升
長天期債券發行量，全球企業今年以來已發行超過 4000 億美元的債券，用於技術投資，而其中大部
分債券發行於美國。另外，各國政府持續的預算赤字，美國聯邦政府債務規模首次突破 40 兆美元，

美國政府需要擴大發債量 ， 投資人要求更高的利率來購買債券等因素 ，皆為推升美國長天期殖利率上
行的原因。因此，長天期債券後續表現仍須持續觀察利率走勢、債券供需變化及通膨發展。
展望 10 月，由於美國通膨仍高於聯準會 2%的目標，2026 年底有再升息一碼的可能，使得短
天期利率下行空間有限。在政策利率可能維持高檔、長天期殖利率又受到供給面壓力推升的環境下，
當前的債券配置應以「息收防禦」與「存續期間控管」為核心。隨著美國公債利率上行，彭博美國投
資級債指數平均剩餘到期期限持續下降 ， 存續期間縮短有助降低利率變動對債券價格的影響 。美國債
市投資建議重點配置中天期美國公債與投資級公司債 。中天期美國公債與投資級公司債不僅能鎖定具
吸引力的收益率水準，也能兼顧存續期間風險控管，降低利率波動對投資組合淨值的衝擊，在當前環
境下有助於兼顧收益與風險控制。

We keep our 3Q26 iPhone build estimate unchanged at 54M units (+4% q/q, -2%
y/y). But we have made adjustments to new iPhone 18 builds by lowering iPhone
Duo by 0.5M units to 0.5M, while raising iPhone 18 Pro Max by 0.5M units to 8.5M.

For 4Q, we lower our iPhone build estimate by 1M units to 72M units (+33% q/q,
-5% y/y), reflecting lower legacy iPhone models (iPhone 16/17e). Total new iPhone
18 model family units remains at 71M units (-21% y/y) in 2H26, with one less SKU
launched compared to last year. We continue to expect 7M units of iPhone Duo
builds in 2H26. With iPhone Duo sales starting soon, we will continue to monitor
ramp-up progress for any upside or downside to builds. Our revised full-year iPhone

build forecast implies CY26 shipment at 234M units (+3% y/y).
Our 3Q26 iPad build forecast is lowered by 0.5M units to 11.5M units (-12% q/q, -
18% y/y). We lowered 3Q entry level iPad builds due to  pull-forward demand in 1H
and the impact of rising prices. We also lower our 4Q iPad build forecast by 0.7M
units to 10.3M units (-10% q/q, -26% y/y). Our revised full-year iPad build forecast
implies CY26 shipments at 46.8M units (-14% y/y).

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

## Document 138: 261015_ubs_TSMC

Source PDF: all report pdf/261015_ubs_TSMC.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ab 15 October 2026
Global Research
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing
Full speed on capacity expansion, backed by a
strong US commitment
Cloud AI demand strengthening, with no signs of slowing
TSMC management remains optimistic of a stronger multi-year Cloud AI mega-trend. Its
optimism is based on its prudent and comprehensive assessment of data centre
construction, power shell build up, component supply, and the financial situation of
Cloud service operators. Its capex target was retained at US$60-64bn in 2026, implying

a meaningful uptick to US$19.4-23.4bn in Q4 vs US$40.6bn in 9M26. Our recent
research highlighted TSMC is further accelerating expansion across N3/N2/A14, and
could lift capex to US$90bn in 2027E and US$105bn in 2028E.
Stronger commitment to  US expansion
TSMC guided the overseas dilution on gross margin to rise to 3-4% in 2027 from a
narrowing to 2% (vs. prior 2-3%) in 2026 given the accelerating expansion especially in
the US. The second fab in Arizona should start mass production for N3 in Q327, slightly

earlier than prior plan. Management also stated that it's in talk with SpaceX/Terafab on
potential collaboration, with key principle being to operate the fabs by TSMC.  As what
we wrote recently, TSMC is also evaluating the possibility to expand outside  Arizona,
with Texas being an option. The greater ambition to expand global footprint and
support reshoring is key in solidifying TSMC's competitive positioning, in our view.
Better Q4 sales guidance, slightly down GM guidance not concerning

Q3 EPS of NT$29.43 was above LSEG consensus of NT$28.67, mostly due to higher sales
and OPM of 58.7%. Q3 gross margin of 66.4% was a bit below expectation of 67%
into earnings, given N2 dilution. Q4 sales is guided to grow 14% QoQ at mid point,
slightly ahead of expectation of ~10% QoQ into earnings, driving full year sales
guidance in USD to grow 44% YoY. TSMC guided greater dilution from N2 and overseas
expansion, which lowers Q4 GM 40bps QoQ to 66.0% at the mid point of guidance. We

view TSMC’s investment case on strong growth at sustainable margins with good
support in 2027  (UBS GMs up 10bps YoY at 66.7% from 66.6% in 2026) with price
hikes to pass on higher costs.
Valuation: maintain Buy and target of NT$3,650
We raise 2026-28E EPS by 2-3% to reflect higher sales even with slightly lower gross
margin assumptions. Our new 2028E EPS is 10% above consensus pre-earnings. We
view resilient risk/reward for TSMC stock at 16x 2027E PE vs 36% earnings growth in

2027 based on UBSe, and expect the stock to steadily outperform the broader tech
sector at least through early 2027, assuming the market volatility persists. Reiterate Buy
rating. Our price target is based on 20x 2027-28E PE with a 26% long-term earnings
CAGR.

Company Description
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (TSMC) is the world's leading dedicated foundry
service provider, with annual capacity of about 32m of 8-inch equivalent wafers as of end-
2024. It operates four 12-inch wafer fabs, four 8-inch wafer fabs and one 6-inch wafer fab in

Taiwan. TSMC also manages two 8-inch fabs and one 12-inch fab overseas under wholly
owned subsidiaries WaferTech in the US, and TSMC China and TSMC Nanjing in China.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We base our price target for TSMC on a PE multiple.
We think TSMC faces a variety of risks, including rapidly changing technology, intense
competition, high capital investment and cyclical demand. TSMC also has a history of paying
stock options to its employees, which could dilute earnings.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

## Document 161: 3443 Nomura20261019

Source PDF: all report pdf/3443 Nomura20261019.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

EQUITY: FABLESS
Global Unichip Corp   3443.TW 3443 TT
Global Markets Research
19 October 2026
Riding the next megatrend of SoIC; maintain Buy
Several key CSP projects in the pipeline to support growth until 2028-29F
Benefiting from SoIC — the next megatrend in advanced packaging, in our view
GUC shares have rallied ~100% since late July (vs the TAIEX up 11%), driven by a strong 
wave of consensus earnings upward revisions (Fig. 11). This is largely in line with our 
observations highlighted in our 2 Aug report that “consensus expectations were way too low.” 
We believe the Street is starting to acknowledge that there is a strong pipeline of CSP projects 
over the next 2-3 years, including Google CPU (GOOGL US, Not rated; revenue sharply 
higher in 2026 with momentum potentially sustaining into 2027F and 2028F as Axion 3 
doubles silicon content from Axion 2), as well as the other top three US hyperscalers’ ASIC 
and CPU projects along with Tesla’s (TSLA US, Not rated) AI5/AI6.5. GUC not only has 
secured projects from top US hyperscalers, but at the same time acquired additional 
meaningful customers with volume globally, e.g. China auto ASIC and US AI ASIC using a 
SoW approach. However, among all, the one that has caught our attention the most is the 
Google TPU v.10 compute die opportunity (though we believe the turnkey deal is yet to be 
finalized) — not only because the addressable size could be significant, but this business 
opportunity might originate from GUC’s G-link 3D die-on-die IP. (Fig. 1 Fig. 4) We raise our 
2026-28F EPS for GUC by 15%-37% to incorporate business upside from non-Axion projects, 
and raise TP to TWD9,600 (from TWD5,800), based on an unchanged 40x target P/E, while 
rolling forward valuation to the 2027-28F average EPS. We are yet to incorporate overseas 
convertible bonds (ECB) in our model/EPS estimates.
The rising importance of SoIC in advanced packaging should benefit GUC, given its 
leading investments in Glink-3D die-on-die IP among design service peers
SoIC is not new, but it was small before 2026 given limited adoption by AMD MI300 chip (AMD 
US, Not rated) and Apple M-series chip (AAPL US, Not rated). In 2026, nVidia’s (NVDA US, Not 
rated) demand for CPO using TSMC’s (2330 TT, Buy) COUPE (EIC on PIC stacking) has further 
lifted SoIC demand. In our June anchor report, we noted that nVidia’s Feynman could become 
the first GPU-on-GPU stacking SoIC (along with SiC carrier) AI chip. Since then, several US CSP 
ASIC projects have also planned to enable SoIC die stacking in their chip roadmap, according to 
our industry checks. GUC’s Glink-3D IP (see Fig. 1- Fig. 4) appears well positioned to ride this 
trend as it has developed this IP along with TSMC’s roadmap and currently a number of CSPs 
are using its IP to build their test chip – which in the long run could prove to be a good stepping 
stone for GUC to win more services, in our view. Besides, GUC’s SoIC IP would also be 
deployed with TSMC’s scale-up CPO customers (e.g. AyarLabs, Fig. 5).

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, 
subject to limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an 
appropriate valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow 
analysis, expected return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated 
target price, defined as (target price - current price)/current price.

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that 
the analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 
'N/A' are not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging 
Markets (ex-Asia): MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and 
by other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

本刊載之報告為台新投顧於特定日期之分析，已力求陳述內容之可靠性，純屬研究性質，僅作參考，使用者應明瞭內容之時效性，

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113) 金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

曜越(3540)：2027 年 Gaming PC 與 AI 工 
建議：評等買進

一、曜越為電競電腦零組件大廠，積極切入AI 高效能工
作站系統 
曜越科技股份有限公司成立於 1999 年 11 月，為電腦機
殼、散熱器(模組)及電源供應器製造商，以自有品牌
Thermaltake 銷售桌上型電腦散熱模組、機殼、電源供
應器，專攻 clone 遊戲玩家市場，近年來公司積極整合
機殼、散熱、電源與系統配置能力，從單一產品銷售延
伸至完整 Gaming PC 系統解決方案，滿足高階 
Gaming PC 的效能與穩定性需求。4Q26 公司將切入
AI 工作站(AI Workstation)新業務 ，拓展地端 AI 運算
市場。2026 年 1~9 月主要產品營收占比，Gaming PC 
35%、電源供應器 33%、機殼 14%、散熱 13%、其他
5%。

二、2Q26 本業疲弱，EPS 0.01 元 
曜越 2Q26 營收 10.16 億元，年減 20.5%，毛利率則從
前期 28.1%升至 30.7%，主要是因記憶體漲價，使毛利
率較佳的客製化裝機業務佔比提升， 2Q26 稅後淨利
96.8 萬元，EPS 0.01 元。 
三、曜越從 PC 零組件品牌轉型為系統整合品牌，並積
極切入 AI 工作站市場  
展望下半年 ，3Q26 營收年減 2.9%， 估計3Q26 稅後 EPS 
0.09 元。4Q 通常為傳統旺季，加上 AI 工作站從雲端到
地端的基本條件已於 2026 年第三季成熟，產品規劃完
成，第四季開始進入市場，出貨量將逐步上升。 
2026 年前九月 Gaming PC 營收佔比已達 35%， 超越電
源供應器的 33%，成為最大產品線，象徵系統整合業務
已成為營收核心。曜越的電競 PC 分為三個價格層次：
入門級 1,599-1,799 美元、進階級 2,599 美元、高階
4,399-4,499 美元。其中核心零組件如機殼、散熱、電源
皆是自製，而主機板、CPU、GPU、SSD 等為外購，透
過美國、德國、澳洲、中國等海外子公司進行在地組裝
與銷售。展望下一階段，曜越將複製電競 PC 的成功模
式，推出五款 AI 工作站與 AI PC 產品線，從入門級 AI
電競 PC（View 380 XL，1,801 TOPS）到企業旗艦雙
系統 AI 工作站（A1500 Dual，16,000 TOPS） ，搭載
NVIDIA RTX 5090、RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 及 AMD 
Radeon AI PRO R9700 等高階 GPU。AI 工作站從雲端
到地端的基本條件已於 2026 年第三季成熟，產品規劃
完成，第四季開始進入市場，2027 年營收貢獻將逐季顯
現 ， 目標2027 年占整體業績一成 ， 主要鎖定AI 開發者、
專業創作者、學術科研及中小企業等應用。這代表曜越
正式從傳統電競跨足企業級 AI 運算、專業開發與數位
創作的地端商機，由於商用工作站毛利通常優於一般消
費級產品，這將是明年拉升獲利結構與擴展新市場的最
大看點。 
四、評等買進理由 
投顧認為曜越已從機箱、電源供應器及散熱等 PC 零組
件，轉型至拓展更高毛利率的電競PC 系統整合與 AI 工
作站業務，使 2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 30%以上，
預期 2027 年營收將成長 15%，估計 2026 年 EPS 0.46
元，2027 年 EPS 1.95 元，給予買進的建議，目標價31
元。(16x2027EPS)。

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -30.55 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dệt may, Tiện ích khí và Bao bì & đóng gói là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt 
nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị  
-2,865 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã VHM, VPB và VIC; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều bán 
ròng ở mức -146 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng -3,351 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua nhưng 
đang duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 1,650 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 2,822 hợp đồng nhưng 
đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,636 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam bước vào tuần đầu tiên ở vị thế thị trường mới nổi bằng một nhịp điều 
chỉnh, khi VN-Index để mất mốc 1.800 điểm và lùi về đóng cửa quanh 1.785 điểm. Đây là 
phản ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" khá điển hình: ngay trong phiên nâng hạng chính 
thức 21/9, chỉ số mở cửa hưng phấn rồi nhanh chóng đảo chiều do áp lực chốt lời, với 
nhóm cổ phiếu Vingroup (VIC, VHM, VRE) giảm mạnh dẫn dắt đà đi xuống, và sức ép lan 
rộng sang bất động sản, ngân hàng, năng lượng về cuối tuần. Điểm đáng chú ý là dòng 
vốn ngoại đã tiếp tục bán ròng ngay sau cột mốc ngày 21/9, dù đã mua ròng trở lại trong 
tuần trước đó. Bối cảnh bên ngoài cũng kém thuận lợi khi Fed vừa tăng lãi suất và phát đi 
tín hiệu diều hâu, chứng khoán Mỹ điều chỉnh, giá dầu neo cao, và lợi suất trái phiếu 
Chính phủ Mỹ tăng vọt. Trong một, hai tuần tới, bên cạnh các yếu tố ngoại biên, mùa báo 
cáo KQKD quý 3 cũng sẽ bắt đầu tác động lên diễn biến thị trường chung.

Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ tăng 
trưởng khả quan trong 2Q26 14% 
YoY, với động lực chính đến từ mảng 
dịch vụ CNTT nước ngoài

Mảng CNTT nước ngoài: Lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ IT phân cực mạnh mẽ do tác nhân 
AI (AI Agentic) s ẽ củng c ố xu hư ớng 
đầu tư vào AI

FPT ở vị thế thuận lợi cho phép 
doanh nghiệp đa dạng hóa công cụ 
AI và tối ưu chi phí vận hành

Khuyến ngh ị MUA với giá m ục tiêu 
86,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Do không còn h ạch toán FPT Telecom (FOX) là công ty con, d oanh thu 2Q2026 của 
FPT giảm mạnh còn 13,810 tỷ VND (-17% YoY). Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ không 
bị ảnh hưởng bởi thay đổi hạch toán đạt 2,568 tỷ VND (14% YoY). Mảng dịch vụ CNTT 
tại thị trường nước ngoài tiếp tục là đ ộng lực tăng trư ởng chính, đạt doanh thu và 
LNTT lần lượt là 9,868/1,538 tỷ VND (16.3% /19.3% YoY).

Sự chuyển dịch chi tiêu từ dịch vụ IT truyền thống sang chuyển đổi số và tích hợp AI 
được kỳ vọng sẽ diễn ra ngày m ột mạnh mẽ khi: i) Sự phát triển của năng l ực tính 
toán giúp kéo giảm chi phí token (đơn v ị năng lực tính toán của AI) và thúc đ ẩy nhu 
cầu sử dụng AI của doanh nghiệp; ii) Các mô hình AI mã nguồn mở gia tăng lựa chọn 
cho người dùng sẽ thúc đẩy chi tiêu

Đối với các doanh nghiệp ứng dụng sâu các mô hình AI phục vụ hoạt động kinh doanh 
như FPT , chúng tôi nhận thấy xu hướng tối ưu chi phí tốt hơn nhờ khả năng sử dụng 
các mô hình AI đ ến từ đa dạng nhà cung c ấp; với chi phí trên hi ệu năng tính toán 
được cải thi ện. Trong k ịch b ản cơ s ở, chúng tôi d ự báo biên l ợi nhuận trước thu ế 
mảng CNTT nước ngoài sẽ ổn định ở mức 15.4% nhờ duy trì chi ến lược quản trị chi 
phí hiệu quả qua đó giúp LNTT 2026/2027 đạt 6,198/7,087 tỷ VND (13.3%/14.3% YoY).

Dựa trên định giá FCFF và P/E, triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các rủi ro có 
thể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu FPT . Giá mục tiêu là 86,000 
VND/cổ phiếu.

Các mảng kinh doanh duy trì ổn định 
giúp lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ 
tăng trưởng nhẹ

Sản lượng điện của REE tăng trưởng 
nhẹ nhờ mức tăng của mảng điện gió 
sau khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 vận 
hành

Mảng M&E ghi nhận giá trị ký mới cao 
trong 1H.2026, t ạo kỳ vọng hồi phục 
mạnh mẽ doanh thu

Mảng cho thuê văn phòng đứng trước 
cơ hội lớn đóng góp vào tăng trư ởng 
nhờ các điều kiện thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
58,100 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Quý 2/2026 giảm nhẹ, đạt 2,432 tỷ VND (-3% YoY/-2% QoQ), trong khi l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ đạt 632 tỷ VND (+1% YoY/-11.5% QoQ), do doanh thu của 
mảng bất động sản suy giảm. Qua đó, REE hoàn thành lần lượt 40%/47.8% kế hoạch 
doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ 2026.

Trong Q2.2026, tổng sản lượng phát đi ện của REE tăng nh ẹ, đạt 2.8 tỷ kWh (+2.3% 
YoY) với đóng góp tăng trưởng rõ nét đến từ mảng điện gió khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 
bắt đầu vận hành; tuy nhiên  sản lượng thủy điện suy gi ảm do tác đ ộng tiêu c ực từ 
pha thời tiết El Nino, đạt 1.1 tỷ kWh.

Với năng lực chuyên môn được nâng cao, mảng M&E ghi nhận những dấu hiệu phục 
hồi tích cực trong giá trị hợp đồng ký mới trong 1H.2026, 5,865 tỷ VND (cao hơn 53.9% 
so với giá trị ký mới cả năm 2025). KBSV dự phóng doanh thu c ủa mảng M&E sẽ đạt 
4,499/5,190 tỷ VND (+28%/15% YoY) trong 2026/2027.

Hội tụ bởi 3 yếu tố: i) Định hướng phát triển kinh tế Hồ Chí Minh trở thành trung tâm 
tài chính quốc tế; ii) Hạ tầng giao thông ngày một được hoàn thiện và iii) Chi phí thuê 
cạnh tranh so với chất lượng dịch vụ của E. Town 6, KBSV dự phóng doanh thu mảng 
cho thuê văn phòng trong 2026/2027 tăng trưởng +14%/6.2% YoY.

Dựa trên đ ịnh giá SOTP , triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các r ủi ro có th ể 
phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu REE. Giá m ục tiêu là 58,100 
VND/cổ phiếu, cao hơn 28.3% so với mức giá đóng cửa tại ngày 15/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q.26 bứt phá nhờ khoản 
thu nhập bất thường của nhiệt điện 
Cà Mau và Vũng Áng

Nhiệt đi ện than duy trì công su ất ở 
mức cao nh ờ nguồn cung nhiên li ệu 
ổn định trong 2H26

Điện khí Đông Nam Bộ (NT1&2 và 
NT3&4) có triển vọng hoạt động 
thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
18,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Quý 2/2026, POW ghi nhận Doanh thu và LNST cổ đông công ty mẹ đạt lần lượt 
20,308 tỷ VND (116.1% YoY) và 3,519 tỷ VND (484.6% YoY) chủ yếu nhờ các khoản thu 
nhập từ chênh lệch tiền khí của nhiệt điện Cà Mau và tỷ giá của nhiệt điện Vũng Áng. 
Với kết qu ả trên, POW hoàn thành l ần lư ợt 65%/420% kế hoạch doanh thu và l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ.

Chúng tôi cho r ằng nhà máy nhi ệt than Vũng Áng trong 2026 s ẽ được duy trì công 
suất huy động ở mức cao nhờ: i) Xác suất diễn ra El Nino tăng cao và dự kiến kéo dài 
đến đầu 2027, ii) Nguồn cung than trong nước được bảo đảm từ các đối tác (TKV, TCT 
Đông B ắc) và d ự trữ than d ồi dào c ủa doanh nghi ệp ghi nh ận ở mức cao trong 
Q2/2026.

Dự án đường dây 500kV Mạch 3 đi vào vận hành giúp giảm áp lực cạnh tranh của các 
nhà máy điện tại khu vực phía Nam, qua đó kỳ vọng doanh thu của NT1&2 trong năm 
2026 đạt 11,104 tỷ VND (5.3% YoY). Đối với dự án điện NT3&4, khoản thu nhập chênh 
lệch sản lượng theo giá công suất do sản lượng điện thực tế thấp hơn so với tỷ lệ Qc 
65% của nhà máy được duy trì sang 2H26 và 2027 sẽ giúp cho dự án ghi nhận doanh 
thu lần lượt 17,848/17,621 tỷ VND doanh thu trong 2026/2027.

Dựa trên định giá SOTP và EV/EBITDA, tri ển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các 
rủi ro có th ể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu POW. Giá m ục 
tiêu là 18,100 VND/cổ phiếu.

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco (AST) 
Tiếp tục mở rộng điểm kinh doanh 
22/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trư ởng tích c ực trong 
2Q2026

Tăng trư ởng khách qua c ảng hàng 
không thúc đ ẩy doanh thu t ại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu

Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng 
trưởng doanh thu mới

Cơ c ấu kinh doanh và hi ệu qu ả vận 
hành hỗ trợ cải thiện biên lợi nhuận

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá mục 
tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nh ận doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 479/92 t ỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng ch ờ thương gia. Lũy k ế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 1,035/221 t ỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác tăng 
bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế gia tăng 
được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi khách, đặc biệt 
tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 182 
điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp t ừ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nh ất và 6 điểm mới tại T2 
Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Qu ốc và Gia Bình ti ếp tục mở ra 
dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, phòng 
chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh đ ược kỳ vọng tác động 
tích c ực lên biên l ợi nhu ận của AST . Chúng tôi dự phóng biên l ợi nhu ận g ộp đ ạt 
64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 48%/17% 
yoy.

Chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với AST , giá mục tiêu 76 ,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh đư ợc 
thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng hơn; 
trong khi tri ển v ọng nhóm khách 
hàng công nghi ệp b ị ảnh hư ởng b ởi 
bất lợi thuế quan

Đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyện và xử 
lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho phép 
cải thiện doanh thu và lợi nhuận của 
mảng này

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 785 
tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An v ận hành từ Q3/2025 giúp 
sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài c ủa các đ ợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ s ở để 
chúng tôi đi ều chỉnh tăng tăng trư ởng tiêu th ụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phư ớc trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế quan 
có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xu ất khẩu truyền thống của Việt 
Nam.

UBND TP .HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng mạnh 
từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh trong thực 
tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự án của doanh 
nghiệp ở mảng này s ẽ vận hành hi ệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và l ợi nhuận gộp 
của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ VND lần lượt trong 
2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với BWE, 
giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với giá đóng 
cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

Quan điểm kỹ thuật 
Xu hướng & Hành động

Chỉ số VNINDEX

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX điều chỉnh trở lại trong tuần qua sau khi gặp thử thách tại vùng cản quanh 1850, 
hình thành cây nến tuần giảm điểm nhưng với phần bóng dưới khá dài cho thấy sự tham 
gia tích cực của lực cầu bắt đáy tại vùng hỗ trợ gần. Trên khung ngày, nhịp giảm mạnh giữa 
tuần đã khiến chỉ số đánh mất đáy ngắn hạn tuần trước và chớm cắt xuống dưới SMA200, 
trong khi phiên hồi phục cuối tuần chưa cho thấy sự cải thiện rõ rệt của lực cầu. Mặc dù 
diễn biến này cho thấy áp lực điều chỉnh vẫn có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng 
tăng ngắn hạn chưa bị phá vỡ, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ nhận được lực đỡ tại vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, tương ứng vùng hợp lưu quanh SMA50, và có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại. 
Kháng cự xa: 1880 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1830 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1750 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1710 (+-15)

### Page 9

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 284: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13026023     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1). 
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 6.2% YoY and 5.1% YoY excluding gasoline in September 
Total card spending growth per household, based on Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card data (monthly, MoM%, seasonally adjusted (SA)) and 
(monthly, YoY%, non-SA, right-hand side (rhs))

Source: Bank of America internal data

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories  
Percentage points contribution to YoY growth by type of retail spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data. See methodology for category aggregation definitions.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at the share of “more discretionary” retail spending categories that are increasing YoY underscores that growth is well 
balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).  
Exhibit 6: Services spending growth has been mostly driven by discretionary spending over the past year 
Percentage points contribution to growth by type of services spending (three-month moving average)

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any 
income cohort… 
Total card spending per household by income tercile and the top 5% 
(three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 8: …though discretionary spending for the top 5% has eased 
some since June 
Discretionary card spending per household by income tercile and the top 
5% (three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.  
Exhibit 9: Necessity spending has gone from a tailwind to a 
headwind across all income cohorts

(three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

a narrowing gap

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories 
Share of spending by the top 5% of households by income across select discretionary categories (three-month moving average to September 2026, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).  
Exhibit 13: 8.3% of Bank of America households had a BNPL 
payment in September

repayment of over $500

(monthly, $ amounts)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The tendency towards greater usage is occurring across income cohorts, though is somewhat more pronounced for lower-
income households (Exhibit 15). However, higher-income households have the largest share of BNPL payments above $500 
(Exhibit 16). Overall, in our view, the increase in the share of BNPL users heading into the season does not appear too 
concerning, though the rate of usage for some BNPL consumers does bear watching.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 302: EcoNews of 12 October 2026 (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 12 October 2026 (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas

The bank

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026ECONEWS

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Stronger trade posture pays off. The EU and China have agreed to
restrain hybrid cars exports to the EU to a level half or less than cur -
rently projected over the next four years, cutting exports by “several
million units” though still allowing them to rise. China also agreed to

drop its tariffs on EUR 4 bn of EU exports and provide easier access to
Chinese rare earths and magnets under a fast-track licensing sche -
me. These announcements were made at the conclusion of EU Trade
Commissioner Maroš Šefcovic’s visit to Beijing, following a recent es -
calation in trade tensions. Meanwhile, France and Germany released
a joint non-paper on Strengthening European resilience, addressing
global macroeconomic imbalances and market distorting practices

calling notably for:
i) Broadening the tools the European Commission may use to address
unfair trade practices (including undervalued exchange rates) or im -
port surges;
ii) Procedural changes to make them easier to use (qualified majority
of member states needed to block rather than authorise them). These
measures will be discussed during the next EU council on October
15-16th.
The European Commission also unveiled a new critical materials pac -

kage involving 46 strategic extraction, processing or recycling projects
spread across 16 member states (including 5 in France). The goal is to
reduce the EU’s dependence on China for critical metals.
EUROZONE
Price pressures are broadening, activity holds up. Producer price in -
flation accelerated further in August (8.3% y/y 1 from 5.8%), driven by
energy. Pressures are becoming more widespread, notably in capital
goods and above all intermediate goods (6.8%). They are partly offset

by non-durable consumer goods, which remain in deflation (-0.8%).
Given the PPI's close correlation with the HICP , this points to further
upside risks to consumer inflation. On the activity front, final September
PMIs confirmed the flash estimates, with the composite index still solid
at 53.1 and the manufacturing index stable at 52.7. The construction
PMI improved but remains depressed (43.4; +0.4 pt). Retail sales edged
up 0.1% m/m2 in August.

France
Modest spread relief as budget positioning starts. The spread between
French and German 10Y yields narrowed to 136 bps 3 on October 9
(mostly thanks to a decline in the French yield). As the Finance Com -
mittee of the National Assembly started discussing the 2027 budget,
the government reiterated its strong intent not to deviate from the
5% of GDP deficit target and to get a budget adopted in time, likely
without a vote. This is the most likely outcome — but not to be im -

plemented before 10 December (given mandatory timelines) — which
effectively removes any incentive for MPs to back unpopular measures
during the process. We can therefore expect heated debates (including
multiple no-confidence votes being tabled but rejected) and a lack in
fiscal discipline. In parallel, the "Rassemblement National" party ("Na-
tional Rally" in English) presented its fiscal strategy, which includes an
alternative budget for 2027 and an aggressive fiscal consolidation path

throughout 2032. The party called for a referendum to constitutionalise
1 Year-on-year
2 Month-on-month
3 Basis points
a “golden rule” that would require a deficit at least 0.5 pp below the
debt-stabilising threshold (roughly 3% of GDP), as long as the public
debt ratio exceeds 60% of GDP . The golden rule would require a budget
balance adjustment of 0.5 pp per year if the fiscal deficit is more than
1 pp above the stabilising threshold. The adjustment measures put

forward lack specifics but likely overstate savings, understate costs
and rely on overly optimistic growth assumptions (despite large fiscal
consolidation, at least 1.7% annually over the next three years — a
pace reached only 4 times in France since 2008, apart from post-pan-
demic reopening, and well in excess of the roughly 1% growth potential
estimated by most international institutions). That said, it is significant
that this party has chosen to fly the flag for fiscal discipline loud and

clear. Other parties should do the same.
Activity is holding up. New vehicle registrations rose 11% y/y in Sep -
tember, confirming a marked acceleration over the past three months
(+9.4% y/y in Q3). In Q3, growth came entirely from electric vehicles
(+112% y/y), whose share of registrations reached an all-time high
(38% in Q3, 41% in September). Export growth continued in August,
both in services (+5.2% y/y YTD) and in goods (+5.6% y/y YTD) — though

driven by prices rather than volume in the latter case. Manufacturing
output posted only a moderate rebound in August (+0.3% m/m; fol -
lowing cumulative declines of 2.8% over the previous three months),
limited by renewed supply constraints in Q3 (which limited produc -
tion at 31.5% of companies; +5 pp compared to H1). Industrial produc-
tion fell (-0.3% m/m) as a result of a decline in electricity production
(-2.4% m/m) driven by the heatwave. Credit growth remains dynamic.

Bank and market financing to NFCs saw steady growth in August (+4%).
New housing loans rebounded slightly in August (EUR 12.2 bn) fol -
lowing a relatively weak July, whilst interest rates continued to rise.
Consumer credit remained buoyant.
Upcoming: Banque de France monthly Business Survey (Monday), Bu-
siness creations in September (Friday).
Germany
Bullish official growth forecast upgrade despite mixed data. The
German government raised its 2026 GDP growth forecast to 1.3%

— the fastest pace since 2017 — from a prior estimate of 0.5%, citing
stronger-than-expected exports and a surge in government spending.
The 2027 forecast was also raised to 1.1% from 0.9%. Industrial pro -
duction was stronger than expected in August (+2.3% y/y versus -1.7%
in July; the highest level since March 2025 and higher than antici -
pated), driven mainly by strong growth in construction output, prima -
rily in public works (+10.5%; to the highest level since November 2021).

By contrast, manufacturing output fell by -0.2% (after +3.3% in July)
due to a decline in transport equipment production (lowest level since
April 2022). Likewise, new factory orders hit their lowest level in one
year in August (-10.6% m/m). However, this decline is mainly due
to a base effect. Export growth remains solid: +4.4% YTD, despite a
-0.8% m/m decline in August. Exports to the Eurozone continue to rise
(+6.3% y/y YTD), while exports to the U.S. have returned to positive

territory (+1.1%) and those to China continue to decline (-12.9%). The
trade surplus reached EUR 19.5 bn in August (+4% y/y YTD, despite a
-9.8% m/m decline).

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026

ECONEWS Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
UNITED STATES
Mild slowdown in services and more rate hikes signaled. The non-ma-
nufacturing ISM survey fell 0.5 pp4 to 54.9 in September due to a sharp
slowdown in the business activity component (down 5.2 pp to 56.5 pp,
still a robust expansion). Employment rose and supply pressures in -
tensified for the first time since June, while the price gauge reached

its highest level (74, up 1.4 pp) since 2022. Meanwhile, the minutes
of the September FOMC meeting confirmed the Fed’s hawkish outlook
and noted that the AI-buildout carried growing weight in the reading of
current inflation data and outlook. Governor Waller and St. Louis Fed
President Musalem mentioned the need for additional rate hikes while
remaining flexible on timing. The New York Fed survey of consumer in-
flation expectations showed them rising to 3.9%. We continue to expect

two more hikes, the next one coming in December.
Upcoming: September CPI (Wednesday), September PPI and Retail
Sales (Thursday), September Industrial Production (Friday).
JAPAN
Solid wage growth fails to lift household spending. Total wage growth
eased to 3.8% y/y in August (down 0.5 pp) as bonus payments stalled
(0% y/y, after 5.3% y/y in July). This allowed continued real wage growth
at 1.5% y/y, extending an uninterrupted run of positive readings in 2026

as administrative measures kept inflation in check. However, this did
not prevent real household spending from contracting in August for the
ninth consecutive month (-3.1% y/y).
EMERGING MARKETS
The IIF reports that nonresident portfolio flows to EMs turned negative
at -USD 26.3 bn in September, after USD 11.4 bn in August, the first net
outflow since June, as a hawkish Fed lifted US real yields and the USD.
Equity outflows reached -USD 19.2 bn, led by heavy foreign selling of

Korean equities, while debt flows turned negative at -USD 7.0 bn as
hard currency demand faded and EM credit spreads widened late in
the month. Further hikes from DM central banks will create additional
headwinds, but EM should remain resilient as long as global growth
persists.
ASIA
China
Yuan policy defence. Coinciding with the Beijing visit of the EU trade
commissioner, the People's Bank of China issued an unusually lengthy
formal statement rejecting claims that the yuan is undervalued and

defending its exchange-rate management by reiterating that it has a
‘floating exchange rate regime’ with reference to a basket of currencies.
It further argued that China's trade surpluses are driven by rising in -
dustrial competitiveness rather than currency under-devaluation and
that exchange-rate adjustments will not resolve structural global im -
balances. Nonetheless, it allowed the yuan to hit a four-year high vs.
the CFETS basket — bringing its year-to-date gain to 5.7%. The PBOC

set the USD/CNY's reference rate at 6.7330, the strongest level since
2023.
India
Monetary tightening. The RBI raised its repo rate by 25bp to 5.5%, its
first hike since February 2023, and shifted to a “calibrated tightening”
stance amidst rising inflationary pressures. The RBI introduced new
measures to stabilise the rupee, including direct dollar supplies to
state-owned oil companies and tighter restrictions on FX derivatives.
4 Percentage points

EUROPE, MIDDLE EAST, AFRICA
Central Europe
Cautious monetary policy stance amid mild inflation uptick. Poland’s
and Romania’s Central banks kept policy rates unchanged at 3.75% and
6.5%, respectively. Inflation accelerated in September, with a mild up-
tick in the Czech Republic and Hungary (respectively 2.5% and 1.6% y/y)
and a more entrenched increase in Poland (4% y/y). Inflation is now
above the official target in the Czech Rep. and Poland, but fuel price

caps in both countries should contain inflation dynamics.
Gulf
PMIs for September point for a firm but uneven non-oil recovery. Both
Saudi and UAE PMIs stood at 55.3, driven mostly by export orders in
the UAE and by domestic demand in Saudi Arabia. On the contrary,
Qatar’s PMI fell again (to 46.7), while Kuwait’s PMI also declined but
remained in expansionary zone (at 52.4).
Türkiye
Manufacturing production contracted by 1.6% m/m in August, the lar-

gest monthly decrease since the beginning of the year. On a y/y ba -
sis, it fell by 2.3%. Both domestic demand and exports seem to have
contributed negatively to this poor outcome. On a positive note, in
September, CPI inflation (1.9% m/m, 27.9% y/y) slowed more than the
market expected, and exports rebounded.
LATIN AMERICA
Inflationary pressures slightly up across the region. In Brazil, headline
CPI inflation accelerated to 4.6% y/y from 4.2% y/y in August (above the

central bank's 4.5% target), partly reflecting the reversal of a tempora-
ry electricity price discount in August. In Mexico, inflation rose to 3.5%
in September from 3.3% in August, while in Colombia it reached 6.3%,
slightly up from 6.2% in August. Inflation remained high in Chile but
was stable at 4.1%. In Peru, it was also slightly up (4.2% vs. 4.1%); the
central bank kept its policy rate unchanged at 4.25% for the thirteenth
consecutive month but maintained an upward bias amid rising inflation

and energy prices.
COMMODITIES
Energy: Limited relief. Continuation of skirmishes in the Gulf of Hormuz
and escalation of the conflict between the Houthis and a Saudi-led
coalition left oil prices fluctuating around $100/b for Brent. However,
the complex security situation has been driving the cost of shipping to
record high levels and the price of physical barrel to early April levels.
Late on Friday, President Trump announced an agreement with Russian

President Putin to release to global markets 4.8 million tonnes of die -
sel (roughly 8 days of European consumption), provided conditions in
refineries allow for it, of which 300,000 tonnes immediately. The White
House also announced a suspension of sanctions on Russia’s exports
of diesel until April 2027. Energy experts expect a de minimis price
impact.
European gas prices (TTF) rose by 11.5% over the week to 82 €/MWh.
EU gas inventories reached 73.6% of full capacities on October 11 th

(the official target is 80% at end October).
Food inflation rose sharply in September. The FAO’s Food Price Index
grew by 5.8% y/y, up from just 3% in August. This was driven by cereal
(+17% y/y) and sugar prices (+15%).

Market to remain choppy: Tech equities were volatile in the past month, with rotation 
toward hyperscaler and internet names. That said , s emi and memory (Korea) 
weakness reflected lower risk appetite due to rising bond yields, AI monetization/ARR 
visibility and L-T AI debates (i.e., Broadcom CEO warned that capital may now be a 
bigger constraint), even as company-specific catalysts continued to be strong. On the 
other hand, the positive was the acceleration of AI model scaling (i.e., Meta’s Muse 
Wonder, Gemini 4, etc). That said, w e view the pullback primarily as a reset in 
expectations rather than the evidence of AI deterioration. Despite light positioning, we 
expect the markets will remain choppy in the near term but would  accumulate low 
valuation & sustainable names  (i.e., HBM, non-TSMC foundries, CPUs, CPOs, etc) . 
In our preferred list, we added CLS, while removing MTK and Hon Hai.  
U.S. roadshow takeaways (week of Oct 5): Investor tone skewed neutral to slightly 
cautious, driven by conservative top ‑down macro views, U.S. election, and ongoing 
debates around the L-T AI demand. Positioning in AI infrastructure has come off peak 
levels. Even  so, many investors would accumulate on pullbacks, with interest 
concentrated in “Made in U.S.” names (INTC, LITE, COHR), U.S. memory suppliers, 
TSMC, and selective bottleneck themes — including ASE, ABF substrates, 
MRVL/SMTC, and Japanese MLCCs. 
OCP & earnings season in focus: The week is anchored by AI/semis — SEMICON 
West, OCP, and earnings from ASML, TSMC, etc. OCP reinforced the industry’s pivot 
toward rack‑scale infrastructure, with inference and agentic workloads driving demand 
across compute, memory, and networking. Scale‑up optics, NPO/CPO, and 800VDC 
power were key themes, while AMD’s CPU outlook and Helios/Helion underscored a 
secular CPU uptrend. Nvidia ’s use of PTFE for NVLink boards further supports our 
positive stance on PTFE (see PCB Spec in 2 H27, Oct 5).  On earnings, ASML 
delivered largely in -line results, GM and OP above consensus with revenue slightly 
miss, and raised full year guidance again and is exploring ways to increase capacity 
by >30% 2028 to fulfill strong customer demand, also extending the order visibility 
beyond 2028. For TSMC, we anticipate an inline quarter. 
September monthly sales/data: TSMC sales -0.6% MoM, slightly ahead. Rubin to 
accelerate and T3 ramp. PC forecast trimmed. Substrate/CCL upcycle with 
pricing/margin expansion. Aspeed revenue +101% YoY. Details on page 5.

GFHK Tech Important Stock Calls  
In the past one month, our key reports and stock calls are: 
Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9, reported date on Oct 13: We expect near-term MTK headwinds 
from TPU v9 delays but remain constructive on MTK/MRVL longer term. Humufish’s redesign could 
push tape-out to Jan -27, MP tape -out to 2Q27 and shipments to 1Q28, while EMIB -T yields could 
recover from 45–50%. Rising TPU allocations to Anthropic could constrain Gemini compute, though 
higher internal allocation in 2H26 may support future models. SemiAnalysis expects Anthropic/OpenAI 
to each add 5 –10GW in 2027 vs. a few GW for Google. For TPU v10 (4Q28 /1Q29), MTK/GUC’s 
Icefish remains the only confirmed mainstream SKU, while Broadcom-led Merope faces AMD/Marvell 
competition, with both potentially testing EMIB-T. 
Michelle Jing on PCB Spec in 2H27, reported date on Oct 5: Some market watchers expect Nvidia 
to shift from PTFE to hydrocarbon resin (HC) for the VR300 NVL576 switch tray. HC—one of the glass 
free alternatives within PTFE hybrid architectures—has only recently passed SI testing and has yet to 
enter PCB MP, leaving it roughly three months behind PTFE in qualification. On the contrary, we 
believe that PTFE offers superior performance for high frequency, high speed designs. With VR300 
NVL576 shipments expected in 2H27 and the final configuration likely set by end 26 —and with a 
leading Chinese PCB ma ker already achieving meaningful PTFE hybrid yields —we maintain that 
PTFE hybrid remains the most probable solution for next year’s switch board. 
Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration , reported date on  Sept 29 : Nvidia’s CPO scale -out switch is 
progressing faster than expected, with FAU yields improving. We forecast CPO switch shipments at 
15k/16k/30k in 4Q26/1Q27/2Q27 and 100k in 2027. For Nvidia/AWS scale -up, CPO and NPO 
continue in parallel, with full NPO adoption expected at NVL576 launch. Feynman is also devel oping 
both variants, with CPO potentially using QM6 + six 6.4T OEs. We shift AWS Trainium 4 launch to 
1Q28 and forecast global scale-up OE demand at 6.5m/42m in 2027E/28E. 
Jeff Pu on NAND Re -acceleration, reported date on Sept 20 : According to TrendForce, NAND 
demand is stronger than expected as U.S. CSPs scale AI infrastructure, with eSSD demand nearly 
2x prior forecasts. We raise NAND contract price growth to +21%/+17%/+15% QoQ in 
3Q26/4Q26/1Q27, while hyperscaler inventories decline. Supply remains disci plined at 15%/21%/20% 
YoY bit growth in 2025 –27. Demand is supported by agentic AI, vector databases and KV -cache 
offloading, where eSSD offers lower cost/bit than DRAM and better power efficiency than HDD. 
Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview , reported date on  Oct 7 : TSMC shares hit record highs on 
expectations ahead of the print and potential Terafab collaboration. We expect largely inline 3Q26 
results, capex and full-year guidance, with softer 4Q26 margin (67.2%). Management should reaffirm 
2026 capex at the ceiling and mid-40s YoY growth, while we forecast 37% growth in 2027 on sustained 
AI/CPU/networking demand and price hikes. Key focuses include margins, long -term capex/U.S. 
expansion, large-reticle packaging and A14. We raise 2026E/27E EPS by 2%/8% on hig her N2/N3 
capacity and operating leverage, and roll to 20x 2027E P/E, lifting TP to NT$2,960.
